- **sharding-strategy**: Use when reasoning about horizontal partitioning of data across nodes for storage capacity and write throughput beyond a single node: the three foundational partitioning schemes (range, hash, directory/lookup), the shard-key choice that determines whether the system scales or hotspots, the resharding problem and how consistent hashing addresses it, cross-shard queries and the joins-and-transactions trade-off, the relationship to replication (sharding partitions data; replication copies each shard), and the failure modes (hot shard, skewed distribution, cross-shard transactions, range-end over… ([jacob-balslev/skill-graph](https://github.com/jacob-balslev/skill-graph/tree/HEAD/marketplace/skills/sharding-strategy) · ★3 · 2 copies)
- **sharding-strategy-selection**: Use when data volume or write throughput outgrows one node and you must shard: choosing key-range vs hash partitioning, fixing a hot shard, designing compound or time-series keys. Not for read-only scaling (use read replicas) or single-machine Postgres table partitioning. ([StevenACoffman/steve-skill-market](https://github.com/StevenACoffman/steve-skill-market/tree/HEAD/plugins/data-systems/skills/sharding-strategy-selection))
